## Twenty-seventh pass (2026-10-07) — review of the oldest core code (items 1–159) and a third Lightfunnels look

Four reviewers read the order and checkout core, auth and tenancy, the public surface, and shipping, payments and jobs. A fifth compared the code with Lightfunnels. A second agent checked every finding against the code: 36 were confirmed and none refuted. The duplicate coupon finding is merged into one item. Defects come first, most severe first; features after them.
- [x] 344. [high] Editing an order's items puts bundle-priced lines back to full price and drops free or chosen shipping (backend fix; no UI change)
  - Where: src/modules/orders/orderItemsEdit.js, src/modules/bundles/bundlePricing.js, src/modules/orders/orderService.js. PUT /orders/:id/items (and /items/preview). createOrder stores a quantity-bundle or mix-and-match line with the tier saving already taken off lineTotalAmount, and records it in lineDiscountAmount (bundlePricing.applyBundleTiers). The edit rebuilds every kept line as `lineTotalAmount: Number(kept.unitPriceAmount) * wanted.quantity` (orderItemsEdit.js:93-98) and never runs applyBundleTiers again. Example: product at 10
  - Fix: In orderItemsEdit.apply: (1) Build kept lines with offerId, isOrderBump and isUpsell copied from `kept`, lineTotalAmount = unit × qty and lineDiscountAmount 0. (2) After the loop, run `const bundleSnapshots = await require('../bundles/bundlePricing').applyBundleTiers(workspaceId, lines, transaction)`. Start discountsSnapshot as `[...bundleSnapshots, ...(order.discountsSnapshot||[]).filter(d => d.kind !== 'bundle')]` 
  - Done: orderItemsEdit.apply copies offerId, isOrderBump and isUpsell onto kept lines, rebuilds them at full price with lineDiscountAmount 0, and runs bundlePricing.applyBundleTiers over all the new lines before the subtotal, as createOrder does. The order's bundle entries in discountsSnapshot are replaced by the new ones; the code or automatic entries stay. lineDiscountAmount is now written on kept and added lines.
  - Shipping: free shipping granted at checkout (shippingSnapshot.freeShippingGranted: VIP tier, referral, pickup) makes every line ship free again, and a bundle tier with free shipping does so through applyBundleTiers. The option the shopper picked (shippingSnapshot.option) is priced again with shippingOptions.choose on the new standard price; when the store no longer offers it the order falls back to standard and the option is cleared, as an added line does (item 277). Hand-typed shipping stays as typed and gets no option.
  - Decisions: the bundle's current tiers apply (as for a new order), so a bundle switched off since loses its saving on the next edit. A quote's order (a QuoteRequest with this orderId) gets no tiers, keeping the merchant's exact prices. A kept line with no offer, price 0 and a label is taken as a free gift and never unlocks a tier. Coupon restrictions (345) and the dropship supplier rate are left to their own items.
  - Verified on port 4150 with a test product at 1000 in a bundle (2 units 10% off; 3 units 20% off plus free shipping), a default rate of 500 and an express option (+300): a COD order of 2 units with express was 1800 + 800. The old code's preview of the same lines gave 2000 + 500; the new preview gives 1800 + 800. Editing to 3 units gave 2400 + 300 (free standard plus express), to 1 unit 1000 + 800, and with freeShippingGranted set, 2 units gave 1800 + 0. All test rows were removed and the settings restored; demo variant 1c7c98bb reserved stock stayed 39.
- [x] 345. [high] Coupon restrictions are not enforced: customer and collection limits are ignored, and a product-limited code discounts the whole order (backend fix; no UI change)
  - Where: src/modules/discounts/discountService.js, src/modules/discounts/couponExtras.js, src/modules/orders/orderService.js, src/modules/orders/orderItemsEdit.js. The discounts API accepts and stores customerRestrictions and collectionRestrictions (discountValidation.js:15-16), but discountService.evaluate (discountService.js:15-44) never reads either. It even takes `collectionIds` and ignores it. So a personal code created for one customer (customerRestrictions=[X]) works at /store/:ws/checkout for any phone, and a code limited to a collection works on any cart. The automatic
  - Fix: In discountService.evaluate, after the funnel check: `if (discount.customerRestrictions.length && customerId && !discount.customerRestrictions.includes(customerId)) throw new AppError('DISCOUNT_NOT_APPLICABLE', 'Discount code does not apply to this customer', 422);` `if (discount.collectionRestrictions.length && !(await db.ProductCollection.count({ where: { productId: productIds, collectionId: discount.collectionRest
  - Done: discountService.evaluate now checks customerRestrictions (a shopper the order names who is not on the list gets 422 DISCOUNT_NOT_APPLICABLE "does not apply to this customer") and collectionRestrictions (the order must hold a product linked to one of the collections; smart collections keep their links in product_collections too). New discountService.eligibleProducts gives the products a product- or collection-limited discount covers (in either list), and the percentage or fixed amount is taken off those lines' totals only, never the whole order; free gifts never count.
  - Same rules everywhere a code is priced: createOrder passes its priced lines, the storefront coupon preview and the shipping quote's automatic discount pass theirs, couponExtras.automaticApplies checks collections the same way, and an order whose lines change after the code was redeemed (PUT /orders/:id/items and its preview, an upsell joined to the order) takes the new amount from discountService.amountForLines.
  - Decisions: an unknown shopper (the storefront preview has no customer) passes the customer check; the order checks it when placed, where the customer is resolved from the phone. The minimum subtotal still counts the whole order. A collection restriction covers products linked directly to that collection, not its child collections. The unused collectionIds argument is gone. The refund quote still spreads the order discount over every line (left as it was).
  - Verified in-process in one rolled-back transaction with two test products (A 1000 in a test collection, B 2000): a 20% code limited to A took 200 off A+B (was 600) and refused B alone; a 10% collection code refused B alone and took 100 off A+B; a code for customer X was refused for Y and gave 450 for X and for an unknown shopper; a staff order with the A code had discount 200 and an order by Y with X's code got DISCOUNT_NOT_APPLICABLE; the item-edit preview gave 400 for A2+B1 and 0 for B alone; a collection-limited automatic 30% gave null on B and 300 on A+B, and an order without a code got 300. On port 4150 the coupon preview for the demo variant said DISCOUNT_NOT_APPLICABLE for codes limited to another product and to another collection, and valid 2500 of 25000 for an unlimited 10% code. The test discounts and their audit rows were removed; demo variant 1c7c98bb reserved stock stayed 39.
- [x] 346. [high] A team Admin (users.manage) can make themselves Owner and then remove the real owner (backend done, UI in frontend-handoff.md)
  - Where: src/modules/workspaces/workspaceService.js, src/modules/workspaces/workspaceRoutes.js, src/modules/team/teamRoutes.js. The owner invites a teammate as Admin with POST /workspaces/:ws/team/invite {access:'admin'}. That gives the workspace_manager role: users.manage and roles.manage, but no billing.manage and no customers.reveal_sensitive. The Admin then: (1) calls GET /workspaces/:ws/roles to get the owner role id, and GET /members to get their own membership id; (2) calls PATCH /workspaces/:ws/members/:ownMembershipId {roleId:<owner 
  - Fix: In workspaceService add assertCanGrant(rolePermissions, req). If rolePermissions includes '*', refuse unless req.tenant.role.permissions includes '*'. Otherwise refuse any p where !req.tenant.hasPermission(p). Call it from inviteMember with role.permissions (this covers POST /members and both /team/invite paths, since 'admin' goes through inviteMember too), from updateMemberRole with role.permissions, and from create
  - Done: new workspaceService.assertCanGrant(rolePermissions, req): a role with '*' only by a caller who holds '*', any other role only when the caller holds every permission in it, else 403 ROLE_ABOVE_YOURS. - [x] 359. [medium] Authenticator (TOTP) second step has no per-account limit and a racy try counter, so it can be brute-forced with the password (backend done, UI in frontend-handoff.md)
  - Where: src/modules/auth/twoFactorService.js, src/modules/auth/authRoutes.js. An attacker has the password (for example from credential stuffing) of an account using an authenticator app. In challengeIfNeeded, the totp branch (line 195) creates a new LoginChallenge on every POST /auth/login and returns before the '5 codes in 10 minutes' count that email and WhatsApp codes get. Each challenge allows 5 tries. verifyChallenge reads challenge.attempts (line 224) before checking the code and increm
  - Fix: In verifyChallenge, replace the read-then-increment with the whatsappLogin pattern: UPDATE login_challenges SET attempts = attempts + 1 WHERE id = :id AND consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() AND attempts < 5 RETURNING user_id, channel, code_hash; when no row comes back, answer TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS or invalid. Before checking the code, refuse with 429 when SUM(attempts) over that user's login_challenges created in
  - Done: twoFactorService.verifyChallenge now counts the try before it looks at the code, in one UPDATE … attempts = attempts + 1 WHERE … attempts < 5 RETURNING (as whatsappLogin does), under a per-account advisory lock, so parallel guesses share a sign-in's 5 tries. Under the same lock it refuses with 429 TWO_FACTOR_LOCKED when the account already has 10 wrong codes in 15 minutes or 30 in 24 hours, summed over all its sign-ins (any channel except the WhatsApp sign-in code, which has its own limits), even for a right code. A right code takes its try back when it consumes the challenge, so attempts counts wrong codes only, and only one of two right answers sent together signs in.
  - Way out: a password reset (link or SMS code) calls the new twoFactorService.forgiveWrongCodes, which zeroes the account's wrong codes of the last 24 hours and closes its waiting sign-ins. The SMS reset did not close them before, so a sign-in that had passed the old password could still be finished after it.
  - Decisions: the budget is per account, not per IP (authLimiter already limits each IP), and backup codes are tried inside it. With 3 right authenticator codes in a million per try, 30 tries a day leaves about 1 in 10,000 a day. Someone with the password can keep the owner out of the second step for up to a day; the owner ends that by resetting the password. Making a TOTP challenge stays free, since the tries are what is counted. No migration.
  - Verified in-process against the database with a test account using an authenticator: the old code let 30 parallel wrong codes on one sign-in all be checked (attempts 30); the new code answered 5 × INVALID_TWO_FACTOR_CODE and 7 × TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS to 12 parallel ones (attempts 5); 4 wrong on a second sign-in, then a burst of 8 on a third gave 1 wrong and 7 TWO_FACTOR_LOCKED (account total 10), and a right code on a fourth was refused. On port 4150 the password sign-in answered channel totp, the right code gave tokens (challenge consumed, attempts 0) and reuse 401; while locked, a new sign-in's right code got 429 TWO_FACTOR_LOCKED. authService.resetPassword with a test reset token closed every challenge and zeroed the count, and a new sign-in then passed (a wrong code then a right one left attempts 1). Test users, challenges, sessions, tokens, audit and notification rows were removed; demo variant 1c7c98bb reserved stock stayed 39.

  - Done: otpService.verifyOtp now counts the try before it looks at the code, in one UPDATE otp_codes SET attempts = attempts + 1 WHERE id = (the newest unconsumed code for that phone and purpose) AND consumed_at IS NULL AND attempts < 5 RETURNING the hash and expiry, as whatsappLogin does.   - Verified in-process against the database with one code and a burst of 60 wrong guesses plus the right one sent at once: the old code checked all 61 (attempts 60) and accepted the right one; the new code answered 5 × INVALID_CODE and 56 × TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS (attempts 5, code not consumed). Wrong, right, reuse gave INVALID_CODE / verified / INVALID_CODE; two right answers together gave one verified and one INVALID_CODE; an expired code gave EXPIRED. - [x] 365. [medium] Postgres queue re-runs any job active for 10 minutes: a long bulk-booking batch runs twice, and an auto-booking cut off by a restart books a second parcel (backend fix; no UI change)
  - Where: /home/user/zimos-Backen/src/core/queue/postgresDriver.js, /home/user/zimos-Backen/src/modules/shipping/bulkShipping.js, /home/user/zimos-Backen/src/modules/shipping/carrierBooking.js. releaseStale (line 136) puts back to 'pending' every 'active' job whose locked_at is older than STALE_LOCK_MS (10 min). No job ever renews locked_at, and attempts are not checked. (1) 'Ship selected' with 500 orders (MAX_ORDERS) books them one by one in shipments.bulk_book, each with a courier HTTP call of 1 to 15 s, so the batch easily runs over 10 minutes. Any worker's poll then hands the same job out again. proces
  - Fix: In postgresDriver.runJob, start a heartbeat: setInterval(() => UPDATE queue_jobs SET locked_at = NOW() WHERE id = :id AND locked_by = :worker AND status = 'active', 60000), cleared in the finally block. releaseStale then touches only jobs whose worker really stopped, and a live bulk batch is never handed out twice. In releaseStale, first mark non-repeatable jobs failed instead of pending: UPDATE queue_jobs SET status
  - Done: postgresDriver.runJob renews the job's locked_at every minute while the handler runs (only while this worker still holds it), so releaseStale only takes back jobs whose worker really stopped and a long "Ship selected" batch is never handed out twice. releaseStale now first fails ("Its worker stopped while it was running; not run again") every stale job that had its last attempt (every io job, and no more endless re-runs of a job that kills its worker) or that is declared `once`; the rest go back to pending as before, so a bulk batch still resumes and marks the order it was booking interrupted. finish only writes the outcome while this worker still holds the job.
  - Auto-booking: the carrier_auto_booking consumer is declared `once: true` with an `onInterrupted` hook (new consumer/processor options passed through registry.js, outbox.js and queue.handle). When a restart cuts it off, carrierBooking.autoBookInterrupted tells the merchant through the existing integration.failed notification and shipment.auto_booking_failed audit row ("The automatic booking stopped before it finished. Check <courier> for this order before booking it.") unless the order has its shipment by then; it is never booked a second time. A booking that throws before the courier call is still retried by the carriers policy.
  - Decisions: the stale age is QUEUE_STALE_LOCK_MS (default 10 minutes, in .env.example), the heartbeat a quarter of it capped at one minute; no migration (the existing attempts and max_attempts columns are used). The BullMQ driver renews its own locks and ignores `once`; left as it was. A stale io job (import/export) is failed rather than re-run, matching the io queue's run-once rule; its own row keeps the state it had. README in src/core/queue documents both.

All test rows (courier account, customer, order, event, jobs, notification, email log, audit) were removed and the API restarted on port 4150; demo variant 1c7c98bb reserved stock stayed 39.

  - Fix: In createOrder, when `awaitingPayment && discountRecord` and the code has a usageLimit or perCustomerLimit: - lock the row with `db.Discount.findByPk(discountRecord.id, { lock: transaction.LOCK.UPDATE, transaction })`; - count pending online orders holding the code: `Order.count({ where: { workspaceId, completedAt: null, cancelledAt: null, [Op.and]: literal("completion_context->'discount'->>'discountId' = " + escape(
  - Done: new discountService.roomFor(discountId, customerId, transaction), called by createOrder for every order whose code (typed or automatic) has a usageLimit or perCustomerLimit. It locks the discount row and counts the open orders awaiting their online payment that carry the code (completion_context.discount, not completed, not cancelled, payment window still open) next to usageCount, and the shopper's own such orders next to their redemptions. No room left: a typed code is refused with the existing 422 DISCOUNT_USAGE_LIMIT_REACHED or DISCOUNT_PER_CUSTOMER_LIMIT_REACHED (message "held by orders awaiting payment" when a hold is the reason), an automatic discount is left off the order. discountService.redeem now also checks perCustomerLimit under its row lock (not for the late redemption of a paid order).
  - Decisions: the holds count for COD and staff orders too, so a COD order cannot take the use an online shopper is paying for. A hold ends when the payment lands (it becomes a redemption), when the order is cancelled or expires, or once its payment window has passed even before the expiry job runs. A payment that lands after its window still records the use (allowOverLimit, as before), so a code can still go over by an order paid late. The storefront coupon preview does not count holds (it answers at placement). No migration; the count uses the JSONB path on orders already filtered to open unpaid ones.

With perCustomerLimit 1, X's second card order and a COD order were refused while Y's COD order was accepted and completed (normal path). A hold whose payment window had passed no longer blocked Y. An automatic 20% with usageLimit 1 gave X 200 and Y's order went through with no discount. On port 4150 the coupon preview for a code with usageLimit 3 and perCustomerLimit 1 answered valid 2500 of 25000. - [x] 368. [low] Anyone can block a merchant's WhatsApp sign-in and SMS password reset by registering a throwaway account with their phone (backend fix; no UI change)
  - Where: src/modules/auth/authService.js, src/modules/auth/whatsappLogin.js, src/modules/auth/authValidation.js. Merchant M verified phone 201001234567, which is also on their store's WhatsApp button. An attacker calls POST /auth/register with any email and phone '201001234567'. register stores the phone exactly as typed, unverified, and the row exists even when sign-up codes are on. Then: (1) whatsappLogin.request (line 40) finds two users with that phone, verified or not, and always returns the decoy, so M silently never rece
  - Fix: Filter all three lookups on a verified phone. whatsappLogin.request: findAll({ where: { phone: normalized, phoneVerifiedAt: { [Op.ne]: null } }, limit: 2 }), keeping the exactly-one rule. requestPasswordResetSms and resetPasswordSms: findOne({ where: { phone: normalized, phoneVerifiedAt: { [Op.ne]: null } }, order: [['phoneVerifiedAt', 'DESC']] }), so the request and the confirm pick the same verified account. regist
  - Changed: whatsappLogin.request now looks only at accounts that verified the phone (`phoneVerifiedAt` not null, limit 2, still exactly one active account or the decoy answer). authService gained findByVerifiedPhone (phone = normalized, verified, newest `phoneVerifiedAt` first), used by both requestPasswordResetSms and resetPasswordSms, so the request and the confirm always pick the same verified account and an unverified sign-up can never get the reset.
  - Decision: register is left as it is. authValidation already normalizes the sign-up phone, and refusing a phone another account holds would tell an attacker which numbers have accounts; an unverified copy of the number is now simply ignored by every phone lookup. No API shape or error code changed.
  - Verified in-process: a test account with a verified phone, then a real authService.register with the same phone (stored unverified). WhatsApp sign-in with `+` and the number made a challenge for the verified account (the old code returned the decoy, confirmed by re-running on the stashed code); the SMS reset changed the verified account's password and left the sign-up's alone. With only the unverified account left, sign-in gave the decoy with no challenge, the reset request stored no code and the confirm answered INVALID_CODE. On port 4150 POST /auth/login/whatsapp/request still answers the masked shape. All test users, challenges, codes, sessions, tokens, notification and audit rows were removed; demo variant 1c7c98bb reserved stock stayed 39.
